### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI 從 8 月的 55.4 降至 54.9，顯示服務業活動增速放
緩，然而國內需求強勁使供應鏈承壓，並推動投入價格指數由 72.6 升至 74.0 為
四年多來新高；另一方面，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 9 月的 48.1
降至 46.3，為連續第三個月下降且創 5 月以來最低 ， 惟未來一年通膨預期由 4.6%
升至 4.7%，顯示通膨可能持續維持高位至 2027 年。
⚫ 聯準會 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示與會官員均支持升息，並認為年底前尚需升息
一次 ，但對於後續升息時點及升息理由上存在分歧； 其中部分官員認為升息是防
止能源及其他價格衝擊造成更廣泛影響 的預防性措施，但更為鷹派官員則認為
升息在於抑制投資和支出，以防範正在顯現的需求驅動型通膨。
⚫ 歐洲央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示全體決策委員均同意升息，並認為當前經
濟成長風險偏向下行 ， 通膨風險則偏向上行 ，因此將保留政策彈性， 根據經濟數

據做為決策，不提供未來利率路徑指引。
⚫ 台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨警
戒線 ， 且創兩年半來最大漲幅 ，除了豪雨導致蔬果價格上漲及中秋假期的短期因
素外，油價居高不下亦使通膨壓力增加。
⚫ 中國央行發布匯率政策立場聲明， 強調中國貿易發展根源於產業國際競爭力的
提升，無必要通過匯率貶值獲取貿易競爭優勢 ，並指出中國長期實施以市場供需
為基礎、參考一籃子貨幣進行調節管理浮動匯率制度。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.699，低點在 31.940，以 31.875 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 4.5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，受到前週公布的美國非農就業數據低於預期，降低
市場對聯準會 10 月升息的預期， 台股創高吸引外資匯入 ， 推升台幣最高來到31.699；
不過隨著美伊緊張情勢升溫且台灣連假將至 ， 台股收低使外資轉為大幅賣超並匯出 ，
推低台幣回到 9 月中以來的震盪區間下緣，最低觸及 31.940，最終在央行調節之下
收在 31.875。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心
指數持續下滑，由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期
升至 4.7%，加上中東局勢反覆，油價高位震盪，仍支撐市場對於聯準會年內升息
的預期，預計美元指數維持在 102 附近震盪，對台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台灣 9 月出口創歷史新高，年增 60.9%，反映 AI 及科技需求持續強勁，出口商後
續拋匯需求可望提供台幣支撐；然而，台股逼近五萬點關卡之際，外資未能延續買
超走勢，且連假前已大幅賣超台股，若後續外資持續匯出，將限制台幣升值空間。
綜合以上，預估本週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪 。 中國十一長假期間 (10/1~10/7) 境內人民幣市場休市 ， 而
境外人民幣維持偏強格局 ， 主要在6.7000 上方震盪 ， 臨近周末 ， 人行中間價為6.7330，
創 3 年多來新高，加上美元指數漲勢放緩，推升境外人民幣突破 6.7000 整數關卡，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.6927，週線升值 134 點，境內人民幣收在 6.6929，較十一長
假前夕升值 121 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間偏升。就國際市場來看，美國密西根大學消費者信心指數
由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創5 月以來新低 ， 不過對於未來一年的通膨預期升至4.7%，
加上油價維持高檔 ， 市場對於聯準會年內升息的預期仍存 ， 美元指數維持高檔震盪 ，
將限制人民幣升值速度；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價維持偏強設定，不過人
行表示將防範短期大幅波動與急貶風險 ， 並無意透過人民幣貶值取得貿易競爭優勢 ，
有助於穩定市場對人民幣的預期 ；最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 各天期
均線仍向下發散，空頭格局不變，下方支撐先看向 9 月底低點 6.6908。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7100

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪走升。週初，因美元指數在102 附近盤整，帶領USD/JPY 延
續前一週走勢震盪走升到 158 上方；週間，因缺乏重要經濟數據，市場偏向觀望，
USD/JPY 維持在 158 附近震盪；臨近週末，USD/JPY 曾一度回測 157.50 附近，不
過在美日利差的主導下，USD/JPY 依舊保持偏多的走勢，終場收在 158.31，週線上
漲 84 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、路透短觀調查顯示，受半導
體強勁訂單推動，日本 10 月大型製造業信心升至正 22，創近五年新高；但原料成
本高漲重擊非製造業，信心指數降至正 23。與此同時，此前投下升息反對票的日銀
鴿派委員佐藤綾野立場軟化，表態支持分階段升息，市場對後續正常化預期升溫，
預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間 。二 、 日本首相高市早苗表示密切關注匯率與物價
走勢 ， 強調日本已脫離通縮 ， 將完全尊重央行政策獨立性 ， 暗示不反對進一步升息 。
面對通膨加劇與日圓走貶壓力，其過往堅持的貨幣寬鬆立場已出現轉向。然而目前
美日實質利差仍大，持續為 USD/JPY 提供支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，
USD/JPY 持續沿著上彎的月線上攻，然匯價持續承壓於季線下方，在有效站上季線
及突破前高壓力前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震
盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：157.10-159.50。 
前週交易區間 
157.41 158.50

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 震盪 走跌 。週初， 因法國債券風險與區域政治不確定性 ，
EUR/USD 下行最低來到 1.1160； 週間 ，受中東地緣風險升溫與歐洲財政疑慮帶動避
險需求 ， 加上美債殖利率維持高檔 ， 美元利差優勢延續，歐元維持低檔； 臨近週末 ，
逢低歐元有買盤支撐，帶動歐元收復部分跌幅，終場收在 1.1201，週線下跌 46 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學
家連恩表示，夏末能源成本飆升為經濟增長帶來下行風險，並可能限制央行收緊貨
幣政策的力道，與此同時，歐元區 10 月 Sentix 投資者信心指數由 5.1 降至 2.7，德
國預期指數同步滑落，反映市場對經濟復甦的信心明顯降溫，預計將令歐元持續承
壓。二、法國社會動盪與支出訴求阻礙財政減赤進程，加劇債務可持續性疑慮，並
推升法德公債利差與信用風險，令歐元承壓走弱。相較美國經濟展現韌性且聯準會
維持偏鷹立場，歐洲深陷景氣放緩與財政掣肘，歐洲央行緊縮空間受限。在歐美基
本面與政策前景持續分歧下 ，EUR/USD 缺乏實質支撐 ， 後市將維持偏弱整理並下探
關鍵支撐。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖多次向下測試 1.1165 關
鍵價位均獲得支撐，惟每次反彈均無法站上五日線，且各天期均線下彎呈現空頭排
列。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1100-1.1260 
前週交易區間 
1.1160 1.1276

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.05-115.10.08 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     2.05% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.82%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週邁入新的提存期 ， 央行連日減發存單 ， 令市場資金供
給充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週一央行標售 364 天期定存單 1100 億元 ， 加權平均得標利率為1.602%， 低於
市場預期區間 1.61%-1.635%，較上期標售結果下跌 4.1 個基本點。 
週 四 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 受美債利率續創新高影響，週四 10 年期指標券 A15109 成交在 2.05%，創同
年期券自 2008 年以來高點，5 年期指標券 A15108 則未成交，最近成交利率
為 1.82%。 
台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨
警戒線 ， 且創兩年半來最大漲幅 ， 目前油價仍居高難下 ， 通膨壓力將令央行維
持偏緊的貨幣政策，加上國際債市利率持續走升，台債殖利率恐面臨上行壓
力，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.95%，10 年券區間為 2.0%~2.10%。

### Page 2

Earnings revision: We factor in Landmark 3Q26 revenues and revise up earnings by 5%/ 
1%/ 1%/ 1% in 2026-29E mainly on higher revenues of CW laser epi-wafer and diodes 
for 1.6T products, supported by rising demand from high-speed optical modules. We 
keep GM and Opex ratio largely unchanged.

Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology (unchanged) and apply a 61.5x 
(unchanged, report link) target P/E multiple on our 2029E EPS, discounted back to 
2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, risk free rate 1.6%, and market risk premium at 
5.1% - unchanged). Our target P/E multiple of 61.5x is derived from the PEG&M ratio of 
peers (unchanged) in the photonics supply chain. Consequently, our 12-month target 
price is now NT$4,459 (vs. previously at NT$4,401). Our new TP implied 2028E P/E of 
76x is between company’s avg. P/E at 62x and avg. +1 P/E at 91x. Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark Optoelectronics Corp.  
Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology, applying a 61.5x target P/E 
multiple on our 2029E EPS, then discount it back to 2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, 
risk free rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), to derive our 12-month target 
price of NT$4,459. Our target P/E multiple is based on the same PEG&M ratio as peers in 
the photonics supply chain. We are Buy-rated on LandMark. 
Key risk: Slower-than-expected SiPh penetration; stronger-than-expected competition; 
Integrated Device Manufacturers (IDMs) offering Epitaxial (EPI) wafers in-house.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

### Page 1

ab 5 October 2026
Global Research
Global I/O
Smartphones
August ’26 Sell-Through: No signs of recovery
yet
Global smartphone unit sell-through -8.9% YoY August '26 YTD
Global smartphone unit sell-through (source: Counterpoint) for the month of August
'26 came in at 85.9m units (-0.1% MoM, -12.3% YoY), marking the ninth consecutive
month of YoY sell-through decline (was -12.2%/-10.9% YoY in July/June). By region, all
regions except for the US (-3.4% YoY) saw double-digit YoY declines, with India seeing

the most incremental downside at -17.7% YoY, followed by China (-17.3%), Europe (-
10.7%) and RoW (-10.1%). Following eight months of significant YoY weakness,
August '26 YTD global smartphone unit sell-through now sits at -8.9% YoY with all
regions well in negative comps (India/China/Europe/RoW/US: -12.3%/-10.5%/-8.7%/-
8.0%/-4.4% YoY).
Apple outperformance continues, Samsung beginning to rebound
Apple: YTD iPhone unit sell-through (149.3m units, +8.7% YoY) continues to highlight

the material outperformance of Apple, which remains the only major OEM (>10% unit
market share) in positive comps. With key iPhone suppliers optimistic on unit growth in
'27 (link) on the back of (1) the change in launch schedule (3 models each in 1Q27 and
3Q27) and (2) the introduction of iPhone Duo (~10m units in first 12 months), we
continue to forecast Apple strength to continue beyond '26 (UBSe sell-in: +8.5% YoY)
and into '27 (+4.7% YoY). Samsung: August '26 sell-through came in at 20.3m units

(+0.9% YoY), returning to positive comps for the first time since March '26, primarily on
the back of the release of the new flagship foldable models (Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Fold8,
Flip8) released on August 7th. New foldable sales in August came in at 2.8m units, only
0.3m units (or 7.5%) below July+August '25 sales for last year's lineup (Galaxy Z Fold7,
Flip7, Flip7 FE; released on July 25 th, 2025) despite two less weeks of sales. Whilst
YTD sell-through still remains soft at -4.1% YoY, we believe resilient sales of the new

flagship model as well as incremental share gains in the mid-range should help Samsung
achieve YoY unit growth (UBSe '26E sell-in: +4.4% YoY). China Android: We continue
to see memory-driven downside materialising with Transsion, Oppo, Vivo, and Xiaomi
YoY sell-through decline now at -23.1%/-19.0%/-18.2%/-18.1%.
Reiterating our sell-in forecasts of -10.0%/-3.0% YoY in '26E/'27E
We entered the year with a cautious view on the smartphone industry (link) primarily on

the back of rapidly rising memory costs. We also flagged that these dynamics will be
much more penalising for Chinese OEMs focusing on the low-end/mid-range segment.
With the DRAM/NAND upcycles likely to extend until at least 2Q28E/4Q27E and unit
sell-through now down 8.9% YoY globally (and on average 16.0% for Transsion, Oppo,
Vivo, and Xiaomi) eight months into the year, we expect to see double-digit YoY unit
decline % and reiterate our '26E sell-in forecast of -10.0% YoY. Heading into '27, with

memory likely to remain an incremental headwind, we expect to see muted demand for
smartphones and see units declining further from a low base. We recently lowered our
YoY unit sell-in forecasts for '27E to -3.0% from +2.0% (link).
Selective on smartphone plays; prefer memory/MLCC suppliers
Within the smartphone supply chain, we continue to prefer MediaTek and Samsung
(both APAC Key Call Buy) as well as ASE, Hon Hai, JCET, Micron, Murata, SEMCO, SK

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
The handset industry, as part of consumer electronics, is cyclically sensitive to the economy
and so upside and downside risks relate to macro drivers. Other downside risks include the
rapid loss of market share due to poor product timing, design or distribution. The supply
chain companies into the handset industry also operate in a highly cyclical market both from
macro concerns and inventory cycles within the supply chain. These companies also operate

in a highly competitive environment with product innovation and R&D investments critical to
success. Valuation methods across the sector include multiple analysis.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.
EXCEPTIONS AND SPECIAL CASES:UK and European Investment Fund ratings and definitions are: Buy:  Positive on factors
such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Neutral: Neutral on factors such as structure, management,
performance record, discount; Sell: Negative on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount. Core

jurisdiction. There is a risk that a loss may occur due to a change in the price of the stock in the case of trading stocks, and that a loss

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

產品組合

股價相對大盤走勢

穩懋 (3105 TT)  Buy

PIN 等光通訊訂單效應持續推升業績向上，評等為 Buy。  
投資建議 ：穩懋 08/2026 營收 19.37 億元， MoM+2.40% ，符合
群益先前預估， 3Q26 營收動能來自 optical 、 infrastructure ，預
估 09/2026 營收仍將較 08/2026 增加。就評價而言，穩懋受惠於
PIN 等光通訊新訂單自 06/2026 開始量產，預估出貨量 4Q26 將
持續放大，且後續成長空間可期，為 4Q26 、2027 年重要營收動
能，故群益維持對穩懋 Buy 的投資建議。  
2Q26 稅後淨利 QoQ+83.22% ： 2Q26 營收 52.57 億元，
QoQ+14.54% ，動能來自 cellular 、infrastructure 、optical 等產品，
此外，雖然 optical 新產品量產初期良率較低，不過，由於 2Q26
產品結構優於 1Q26 、產能利用率提高，毛利率提高至 28.24% ，
2Q26 營業利益 QoQ+72.14% ，加上認列 1.44 億元匯兌利益、
2.70 億元投資利益， 2Q26 稅後淨利增加至 9.77 億元，
QoQ+83.22% 。  
就 2Q26 的產品結構而言， cellular 產品營收 QoQ+18.00% ，原
因在於手機業者需求隨逐漸進入旺季而增溫； infrastructure 產
品營收 QoQ+14.53% ，動能來自 AI data center 、低軌道衛星；  
optical 產品 2Q26 重要動能有二， (1) 新款 iPhone 相關 VCSEL
訂單數量增加， (2)PIN 等光通訊新訂單自 06/2026 開始量產。  
PIN 等光通訊新訂單出貨量放大為後續重要營收動能： 展望
3Q26 ， optical 產品受惠於 PIN 等光通訊新訂單出貨量放大，營
收成長性居各產品之冠，預估將帶動 others 產品營收
QoQ+35.97% ，且後續成長空間仍可期；其次， infrastructure 產
品出貨動能來自低軌道衛星標案陸續出貨，預估相關營收
QoQ+20.45% ，由於全球各地低軌道衛星標案隨應用領域拓展而
有機會持續釋出，後續營收前景亦仍佳。  
至於 cellular 、 Wi-Fi 產品出貨成長性雖受到近期記憶體缺料等
因素影響整體手機產業景氣而存在不確定性，但現階段預估營
收均與 2Q26 實績相去有限。

2 群益投顧 
  企業 ESG 資訊揭露：  
就企業溫室氣體排放量減量之預算與計畫而言，穩懋持續透過各項節能措
施提升廠內能源使用效率，以減少用電度數，且新設產線全面採用最佳可行
技術，實際措施包括 PFCs 尾氣處理設備全面採用去除效率達 90% 以上之型
式 (IPCC 公告指定型式 )、逐步汰換既設產線之 PFCs 尾氣處理設備、評估製
程氣體減量使用或取代高潛勢溫室氣體使用、採取工程改善或設備更換以
降低用電量、評估新建廠房設置廠內再生能源等。  
再就企業產品或服務帶給客戶或消費者之減碳效果而言，穩懋致力於新技
術的開發，持續推出高效能的技術平台，以生產更具能源效益和節能的半導
體通訊產業產品，因終端產品應用逐漸擴及手機、無線網路、雷達、衛星等
各個通訊領域，有助於全球能源節省和減少用電消耗。此外，藉由穩懋多功
能的晶片整合、製程線寬的縮減以及製程能力的提升，終端產品之尺寸、單
位晶片使用量均持續減少，亦得以為守護地球環境盡一份心力。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 099: 8月海外资金重返美债市场，英国持仓首破万亿，中日继续减持、中国持债08年来新低

Source PDF: all report pdf/8月海外资金重返美债市场，英国持仓首破万亿，中日继续减持、中国持债08年来新低.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

据美财政部报告， 8 ⽉海外投资者净买⼊美债 1089 亿美元，持债规模扭转两⽉连降；⼗⼤
持债国家地区中， 8 ⽉只有中⽇持仓下滑，头号 “ 债主 ” ⽇本在⼲预汇市当⽉持仓创 22 个⽉新
低、连降四个⽉，中国持仓连降三个⽉，⼀年来减少 1090 亿美元，第⼆⼤ “ 债主 ” 英国持仓
两⽉连创历史新⾼，加拿⼤ 8 ⽉增持居⾸、增幅 383 亿美元，总持仓排第五，增幅第⼆位⽐
利时增 198 亿美元。
摘要：
美国官⽅数据显⽰，今年 8 ⽉，海外资⾦重返美国国债市场，但主要海外持有国家地区的配置⽅
向仍然分化，前三⼤ “ 债主 ” 中，⽇本和中国⼤陆继续减持，英国持仓续创新⾼。
美国财政部于美东时间 16 ⽇周五公布国际资本流动报告（ TIC ），披露 8 ⽉海外投资者净买⼊美国
国债 1089 亿美元，扭转 7 ⽉净卖出 36 亿美元的抛售势头。按持仓余额统计， 8 ⽉海外投资者持有
的美国国债总规模环⽐增加 833 亿美元，升⾄ 9.3317 万亿美元，在两个⽉连降后反弹。
美债的主要持有国家地区中，英国持仓规模史上⾸次突破 1 万亿美元，加拿⼤、⽐利时也⼤幅增

持。但海外资⾦重返美债市场，并不意味着所有主要持有国都在增加配置。⽇本的持仓⾃ 5 ⽉以
来持续下滑，和⽇本政府抛售美元资产买⼊⽇元的⼲预⾏动期重合，中国⼤陆则延续了⻓期减
持的趋势，⼀年来减少上千亿美元。
⽇本汇市⼲预规模创纪录，美元资产配置受关注
美国财政部 TIC 数据显⽰， 8 ⽉⽇本持有的美国国债规模环⽐ 7 ⽉减少 356 亿美元，降⾄ 1.0683 万
亿美元，仍是美国最⼤的海外 “ 债主 ” 。
据 TIC 报告，⽇本的美债持仓已连续四个⽉下降。此前 6 ⽉、 7 ⽉持仓连续创 2025 年 1 ⽉以来新
低， 8 ⽉进⼀步降⾄ 2024 年 12 ⽉以来最低⽔平。
⽇本持仓持续缩⽔之际，⽇本政府今年多次⼲预外汇市场、试图⽀撑⽇元，市场因此关注汇市
⼲预是否影响⽇本的美元资产配置。
⽇本财务省 5 ⽉ 29 ⽇公布的数据显⽰， 4 ⽉ 28 ⽇⾄ 5 ⽉ 27 ⽇期间，⽇本外汇市场⼲预总额达到
11.73 万亿⽇元。分析⼈⼠认为，⽇本买⼊⽇元、卖出美元的交易可能涉及⼀定规模的美国国债

出售。
此后，⽇美两国还罕⻅地联⼿⼲预汇市。⽇本财务省 8 ⽉ 3 ⽇证实，⽇本政府与美国财政部于 7 ⽉
31 ⽇协调实施了买⼊⽇元的汇率⼲预，以遏制⽇元汇率近期的剧烈波动。这是⽇美两国⾃ 2011
年东⽇本⼤地震后⾸次协调⼲预汇市，也是⾦融危机等特殊时期之外极为罕⻅的联合⾏动。
⽇本财务省随后公布的数据显⽰， 7 ⽉ 30 ⽇⾄ 8 ⽉ 26 ⽇期间，⽇本政府共计投⼊约 15.4 万亿⽇元
⼲预外汇市场，创下单⽉⼲预⾦额新⾼。
⼲预汇市通常需要卖出外汇、买⼊⽇元。由于⽇本外汇储备中持有⼤量美国国债，市场因此关
注，⽇本是否会通过出售美债等美元资产为⼲预⾏动提供资⾦。
不过， TIC 数据并不能直接证明⽇本为⼲预汇市出售了多少美债。持仓余额的变化既受到实际买
卖影响，也可能受到债券价格变化等估值因素影响，不能将⽇本 8 ⽉持仓减少 356 亿美元全部归
因于汇市⼲预。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

中国持仓连降三个⽉  两⽉连创⼗⼋年新低
据 TIC 报告，中国⼤陆 8 ⽉持有的美国国债规模环⽐减少 273 亿美元，降⾄ 5907 亿美元，连续两
个⽉创 2008 年 8 ⽉以来新低，继续位居美国第三⼤海外债主。
截⾄ 8 ⽉，中国⼤陆的美债持仓已连续三个⽉下降，最近 12 个⽉内，有⼗个⽉持仓环⽐下滑。以
TIC ⼀年来的数据计算，截⾄ 8 ⽉末，中国⼤陆所持美国国债相⽐去年同期⼤幅减少 1090 亿美
元。
从更⻓周期看，中国⼤陆持有的美国国债规模已较过去明显下降。 2016 年前后，中国⼤陆持仓
曾超过 1 万亿美元，如今已降⾄约 5900 亿美元，反映出官⽅持仓持续收缩的趋势。
与此同时，市场近期也出现关于中国可能通过第三地持有部分美国债券的讨论。据本周三的报
道，华尔街部分投资者和分析⼈⼠怀疑，中国可能通过加拿⼤等地的⾦融机构持有部分美国债
券。相关讨论与加拿⼤近年来美国国债及相关机构债券持仓增加有关。
但这⼀说法尚⽆确凿证据。加拿⼤持仓上升并不能直接证明其中包含中国转移的资产，也可能

受到其他投资者配置变化的影响。
因此，中国⼤陆持仓持续下滑是 TIC 数据能够确认的趋势，但仅凭公开持仓数据，尚不能断⾔中
国实际持有的全部美国债券规模同步下降。
加拿⼤增持居⾸，⽐利时持仓也⼤增
在 TIC 报告的⼗⼤海外持有美债国家地区中， 8 ⽉只有⽇本和中国的持仓减少。总持仓规模介于
中⽇之间的英国连续两个⽉增持。
数据显⽰，英国持有的美债规模环⽐增加 112 亿美元，升⾄ 1.0095 万亿美元，有纪录以来⾸次突
破 1 万亿美元，连续两个⽉持仓创最⾼纪录，继续稳居 TIC 报告的海外 “ 债主 ” 榜单次席。
加拿⼤和⽐利时的增持幅度更为突出。加拿⼤ 8 ⽉美债持仓环⽐增加 383 亿美元，升⾄约 4646 亿
美元，增幅在美国⼗⼤海外持有国中居⾸，总持仓排名第五。
⽐利时持仓环⽐增加 198 亿美元，升⾄约 4905 亿美元，增幅仅次于加拿⼤，总持仓排名第四。
值得注意的是，市场近期也在讨论第三地托管数据是否完整反映最终投资者的⾝份。美国财政
部的 TIC 数据主要依据托管机构和交易商报告编制，如果外国投资者通过第三国托管机构持有美

国证券，相关资产可能被记在托管地名下，⽽⾮最终所有者所在国家。
因此，单个国家持仓增加并不⼀定完全对应其本国投资者的实际买⼊。尤其在分析中国等⼤型
持有国的美债配置变化时，托管地与最终所有者之间的差异值得留意。
海外私⼈资⾦流⼊占主导，美债需求能否延续
整体来看，中⽇以外的主要持有美债地区 8 ⽉有不同程度的增加。
在美国⼗⼤海外美债持有国家地区中，总持仓第六位的开曼群岛持债⼩幅增加 29 亿美元⾄ 4630
亿美元，第七位的卢森堡持仓增 61 亿美元⾄ 4482 亿美元，第⼋位的法国持仓增加 150 亿美元、
增幅仅次于⽐利时，增⾄ 3634 亿美元，第九位的爱尔兰持仓增 19 亿美元⾄ 3521 亿美元，中国台
湾地区持仓增 23 亿美元⾄ 2984 亿美元，位居第⼗。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

2578 亿美元，外国官⽅机构净买⼊ 429 亿美元。若进⼀步计⼊短期证券、银⾏资⾦流动等项⽬，
当⽉美国录得 1998 亿美元的净国际资本流⼊。
这些数据表明， 8 ⽉海外资⾦整体仍在流⼊美国⾦融市场，美债需求也有所恢复。不过，海外资
⾦回流与主要官⽅持有国减持并不⽭盾：不同投资者的配置策略可能不同，私⼈投资者的买⼊
也可以抵消部分官⽅机构的减持。

市⼲预是否继续影响其美元资产配置，以及中国⼤陆官⽅持仓下降在多⼤程度上反映实际减
持。
海外资⾦回流为美债需求提供了⽀撑，但⽇本、中国继续减持，也意味着不能仅凭单⽉总持仓

回升，就认定所有主要海外持有者对美国国债的配置意愿都已同步改善。

## Document 175: Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-18)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-18).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#trade #australia
Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August
By Ryan Wells
Economist, Westpac
13:19 October 01 2026 Share
Another surge in data centre-related equipment imports in August, alongside continued
volatility in gold trade. Trade balance: $0.5bn surplus. Export values: 3.7%mth. Import
values: 5.8%mth.

### Page 2

Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August from $1.4bn in July (revised down from
$1.9bn previously). This result was significantly lower than expected – the market consensus was for a
broadly stable reading, while Westpac had a slight widening pencilled in. Over the past six months or
so, the trade balance has been oscillating between small deficits and surpluses, largely coinciding with
significant month-to-month volatility in the trade mix. The chief culprit behind this volatility has been

trade in non-monetary gold, among other components.
Before discussing the detail, it is once again worth noting the results for gold up front. In August, non-
monetary gold exports jumped 20%, while imports surged 91%. Excluding gold, the trade figures have
been much more stable, closer to a deficit of $2-3bn per month over the past three months, down from
larger deficits around the start of the year.
Export earnings rose 3.7% in August, bouncing back from a 3.6% decline in July. The trend for exports

so far this year looks a bit more positive, but again, this is tricky to read given the volatility present in
the data. Excluding gold, export earnings were up 1.6% in August. This was largely supported by a
4.7% lift in coal and a 2.8% increase in LNG, with further detail provided by the ABS suggests that an
increase in both volumes and prices contributed. However, iron ore (–1.5%) and rural goods (–1.0%)
weighed on earnings in the month.

### Page 3

The import bill meanwhile increase by 5.8% in August. Once again, a surge in automatic data
processing (ADP) equipment imports – which capture the investment upswing related to data centres –
were in the spotlight, surging 79% in the month up to $4.3bn. There were also an increase in other
capital goods imports centred on telecommunications equipment (+12%) and civil aircraft (+30%) –
large in their own right but dwarfed by the surge in ADP imports in the month.

Our latest estimates have the total pipeline for data centres at around $175bn over the coming decade,
with much of that being front-loaded. It is natural to expect month-to-month volatility in ADP equipment
imports as these large and lumpy data centre projects are worked through, but it is worth highlighting
that August’s jump closely resembles the surge from March, when imports also surged to around
$4.3bn. Given the scale of the increase in August, Q3 is shaping up to be another quarter where data

centre investment will play a significant role in driving growth – while it is true that the data centre build-
out is import-intensive, our modelling suggests that once spending enters Australia, the local economic
impact is likely to be around half of the total investment.

### Page 4

Elsewhere across the import basket, while gold imports also surged, it was effectively offset by a
decline in consumer imports of non-industrial transport equipment (i.e. cars), down 21.2% in August
following the surge from May to July. While still tentative at this stage, it suggests that the momentum in
EV purchases, which played a significant role in the consumer spending story in Q2, may be starting to
wane. Additionally, a further pull-back in fuel imports (–5.0%) in August has extended the downward

trend in recent months, following the initial surge in oil prices in March and April after the war in the
Middle East began. However, with the conflict re-escalating recently and margins on refined fuel
products widening, we could see this trend reverse over the coming months.

### Page 5

## Document 192: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

## Document 220: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS
Eurozone sectoral PMI indices: production and new export orders are strengthening across more sectors

The bank for

Chart of the Week / 14 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com
tarik.rharrab@bnpparibas.com
With the help of Ece Usta (intern).

## Document 237: GFHK - UMC update

Source PDF: all report pdf/GFHK - UMC update.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Meaningful Re‑Rating Path into 2H26–2028

Maintained  Buy 
 Target price NT$215

Raise TP to NT$215: UMC’s share price underperformed Taiex in July/Aug but rebounded on 
rising expectations of price hikes and optical opportunities ( our report : PIC/TFLN Upside  
Unlocks a Higher -Margin Cycle  on Aug 24 ). We continue to expect UMC to be re ‑rated, 
supported by profitability optimization through 2H26–2027 (higher UTR, price hikes, improving 
mix, etc), followed by a shift to a new growth phase from 2028 driven by FinFET 12nm and an 
“optical full‑solution model”. Reflecting stronger pricing and margins, we lift 2026E/2027E EPS 
by +2%/+8% and raise our target multiple to 25x 2027E EPS on a stronger margin/growth 
outlook, resulting in a new TP of NT$215. Our model indicates UMC should maintain solid 
FCF and dividend yields. 
Earnings optimization in 2H26–2027 amid transfer‑order gains: We see UMC is in a phase 
of profitability optimization supported by rising UTR, pricing, and improving mix. We expect 
UTR for: 1) 12” to reach ~93% by end ‑2026 on strong DDIC volumes from Novatek and 
Samsung LSI at 22/28nm and Apple’s Mac  at 55/65nm, with upside from Sony’s sensors 
(>10KPM at 22/28nm); 2) 8” to ~90% by end ‑2026 and full loading by 2H27  on intact 
demand (datacenter PMIC, MCU, embedded memory, etc), Samsung LSI’s scale ‑down in 8” 
driving PMIC order transfer (~70KPM in 2027, ~110KPM in 2028), and additional transfer from 
Novatek (HV) and MTK (BCD), with upside from Qualcomm PMIC in 2027. Meanwhile, pricing 
momentum extends from 2H26 (selective customers) into 1H27 (10 –30% across 12” and 8”) 
and 2H27 (ongoing negotiations), suggesting visibility extending into 2H27.  
Transformation from 2028 to “asset‑light FinFET” and “optical full solution”: We expect 
UMC’s next growth phase, likely beginning 2028, to be driven by: 1) Intel 12nm as a major 
inflection—an asset‑light FinFET model with revenue contribution from 2028, plus upside from 
further 12nm expansion tied to Apple’s DDIC migration amid “Made in U.S.”; 2) SiPh/TFLN 
scaling toward 2.4T/3.2T, with TFLN as the largest long ‑term opportunity. We estimate EPS 
contribution of NT$0.7/NT$1.6 in 2027E/2028E, supported by higher ASPs of ~$4.5k and 
wafer volumes of 20KPM/40KPM by end‑2027/2028; 3) interposer and advanced packaging 
beginning to contribute from 2H27 (interposer), supported by emerging vectors such as silicon 
capacitor/DTC, hybrid bonding, and controller ‑to‑bonding integration, positioning UMC to 
evolve beyond PIC foundry toward a broader optical integration model. 
Risks: 1) Geo-political risk; 2) Demand uncertainties; 3) Competition; 4) New product delay.

Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk 
Henry Huang 
SFC CE No. BYA749 
henryhuang@gfgroup.com.hk

Valuation and Recommendation

its earnings volatility has reduced since 2021 due to a much more diversified application mix and 
specialty position. We adopt the 2027 P/E valuation is due to the improving pricing environment in the 
longer term. Overall, we believe the  mature node industry outlook has become more favorable , and 
UMC’s mgmt. also guided a favorable pricing environment in 2026/2027.

networking, power, silicon photonics, advanced packaging) . We expect its ROE to reach mid -20s in

## Document 241: How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

SPECIAL EDITION | HOW BAS IS THE RISE 
IN BOND YIELDS?

TRANSCRIPTION

0:00:10 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Everyone, welcome. As we head into autumn 2026, interest rates are reaching levels never seen for many, many years. 
Why is this happening? Is it sustainable? And what are the implications for the economies of different countries? All these 
questions will be discussed over the next few minutes with the economists of BNP Paribas. Let us begin with Isabelle 
Mateos y Lago, chief economist of the group. Hello, Isabelle.  
0:00:38 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everyone. 
0:00:39 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you for being with us today. Just to begin, a simple explanation. What rates are we talking about and which countries 
are affected? 
0:00:48 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, it is very important to clarify this. So, we are talking about long-term interest rates, rates at 10 years or longer 
maturities, 30 years, etc. The rates at which sovereign states borrow on the market. And this is primarily something that is 
happening in advanced countries, so United States, United King dom, Japan, and then the Eurozone countries. And indeed, 
as you said, we are seeing since the beginning of the summer, interest rates reach levels we had not seen for about 20 
years. But it is important to keep in mind that up until that point, up until the mid -2000s, the level of interest rates that 
we're seeing now were really quite common, quite normal, so one shouldn't overdramatize.  
0:01:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why do we find ourselves in this situation and how did we get to this point?  
0:01:41 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, so the direct cause is that investors are demanding higher interest rates in order to  lend their capital to governments. 
But why is this? Well, there's essentially three reasons for this, and not all of them are bad, and they're not mutually 
exclusive either. The top reason to my mind is that there's just greater competition for capital be tween sovereign states 
and the corporate sector, who are all facing simultaneously additional investment needs, in defense, in artificial 
intelligence, in energy transition, in resilience. And so,  you just have greater competition for capital and that pushes up the 
price of capital, which is the interest rate. 
Reason number two is because markets have revised upwards their expectations of how high central banks are going to be 
taking their interest rates. 
And this mechanically translates into higher rates at the long end. And then there's a third reason, which doesn't apply 
necessarily in every case, but which is essentially higher sovereign risk, a kind of a risk premium, in cases where there are  
concerns arising about the sustainability of these public debts.

### Page 2

2

0:02:59 - Emmanuel Laborde 
What are the implications for global economies? 
0:03:03 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
So that's exactly what we'll be discussing in the next segments. But basically, the two key issues are, one, an adverse 
impact on public finances. It's higher interest rates making it harder to balance the books in terms of public accounts. And 
then secondly, these higher rates tend to slow down economic growth. Of course, by how much and how serious it will be, 
depends on how high interest rates go and how long they stay there. But that's for the next segments.  
0:03:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Yes. Thank you very much, Isabelle. Having said the scene now, let's move on to the first segment with the following 
question. What makes debt sustainable? And let's see what Hélène Baudchon has to say.  
Hello Hélène and welcome into this special edition. 
0:03:58 - Hélène Baudchon 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everybody. 
0:04:01 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When economists talk about public debt, they often refer to the sustainability of debt. As an economist, can you explain to 
us what that means and what is the debt trajectory deemed unsustainable?  
0:04:15 - Hélène Baudchon 
To begin with, I will say that a temporary increase in debt can be seen as a normal evolution, not particularly concerning, 
for instance during an economic crisis or more recently during a health crisis. In such cases, it acts as a buffer. A problem  
arises when the debt level never comes back down. An unsustainable trajectory can be therefore defined, characterized by 
a continuous regular rise in the debt level, both in absolute terms and as a proportion of GDP, driven by high and recurring 
fiscal deficits. And sometimes interest payments can exacerbate the problem.  
0:05:00 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In very simple terms, you are talking about the snowball effect.  
0:05:03 - Hélène Baudchon 
Exactly explaining why consolidation measures are needed in order to prevent such an adverse situation from occurring 
and in order to prevent, in fact, a sudden loss of confidence from creditors.  
0:05:16 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Regarding governments, what should serve as a warning sign for them? And at what point could the situation become, let's 
say, problematic? 
0:05:27 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, simply speaking, when interest rates rise, not so long ago, up until 2022, Eurozone countries were borrowing at very 
low rates and in some instance, even negative rates. And although debt levels were already at high level at that time, the 
low rates helped manage debt servicing. Not much fiscal effort was needed to keep the public debt ratios in check.  
0:05:55 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Of course, we all remember this because it was a very unusual situation, but now things are very different.

### Page 3

3

0:06:01 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed, the situation today is clearly more challenging simply because bond yields are significantly higher and they 
are rising at the time of our discussion. This is for the part the result of the ECB's monetary tightening in 2022 -2023 aimed 
at addressing the inflation shock then. Then long-term interest rates have risen as a consequence and have aligned more 
closely with GDP growth rate. And in technical terms, this is what we refer to as a narrowing of the "r" - "g" spread. 
0:06:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why is this spread so important? Why do you, the economists, monitor it this way?  
0:06:48 - Hélène Baudchon 
Essentially, you have the interest rate "r", which determines the debt burden, while the growth rate "g" determines the 
trajectory of fiscal revenues. And if "r" is lower than "g", and this has been the case for many years, as you can see on the  
chart on the screen, it means that interest payments are increasing at a slower pace than the revenue component of the 
equation. 
0:07:14 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this provides some leeway in other budgetary areas, such as education, of course, defense, maybe pensions.  
0:07:22 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed. And this is what we refer to as the primary budget. However, if and when interest rates do go up while the 
economy isn't growing that much faster, then fiscal leeway diminishes.  
0:07:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Crystal clear. Now, just could you provide a few last words to wrap up the sustainability segment?  
0:07:43 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, to conclude, we are entering a period which demands greater fiscal discipline or, as some might say, less indiscipline 
in order to manage debt trajectories effectively, in particular in the countries which have high debt levels. And for instanc e, 
the Italian case is an interesting one because it is well known for its high debt level ratio. But in fact, it also has an 
advantageous position because it has no primary deficit. In fact, it even benefits from a large primary surplus.  
0:08:22 - Emmanuel Laborde 
As for the countries... For the other countries... I stop you because this will be a discussion with Guillaume Derrien during  
the next segment. Thank you very much, Hélène. 
0:08:30 - Hélène Baudchon 
Thank you, Emmanuel. 
0:08:36 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Guillaume. Welcome. 
0:08:37 - Guillaume Derrien 
Thank you. 
0:08:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Emmanuel, and hello to everyone. The flexibility of fiscal policy depends heavily on interest rates, which have 
increased considerably over the past few months. The question is, where do we stand today?

### Page 4

4

0:08:52 - Guillaume Derrien 
So, long-term rates have increased in all advanced economies, reaching levels not seen since 2008 in Europe and in the 
United States, and even since 1996 in Japan. Fortunately, the effective rate that states pay on their debt is lower than thos e 
headline figures suggest, simply because the average maturity of debt is relatively long. Precisely how long are we talking 
about? So, it's about 10 years in Japan, 13 years in the United Kingdom, and 8.5 years in France. That means that most of 
the debt was raised before rates started climbing. 
For example, in France, the effective rate just recently rose above the 2% mark, which is significantly lower than the 10 -
year bond yield, which exceeded 4% for new issuances.  
That said, in the United States, which has an average maturity of roughly six years, is experiencing a more rapid increase in  
its effective rate. 
0:09:54 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this rate increase is happening in the context of high debt -to-GDP ratios and with modest growth, correct? Yes, that's 
correct. 
0:10:05 - Guillaume Derrien 
Debt levels have reached unprecedented heights since the end of World War II. And at the same time, growth is lower, 
hovering around 2% on average in the United States, 1% in Europe and 0.5% in Japan. What does that mean for debt 
servicing costs? So, debt servicing cost is already relatively high, but it is expected to grow further as debt issue before the 
2022 rate hikes mature and are replaced with new instruments bearing higher interest rates. By 2030, interest costs could 
rise to levels last seen in 2000, specifically in France, around 4% of GDP. 
Are all the countries equally affected by these trends? Actually, no. Some countries are managing to reduce their debt 
level. 
Spain, Italy, even Japan have sufficiently improved their primary balance to achieve this, at least for the time being. And o f 
course, we must speak about Germany. Yes, Germany is implementing investment programs that result in a higher public 
deficit and debt-to-GDP ratio. But this ratio started from a relatively low base at around above 60% of GDP. That said, in 
this current context, German bonds have risen. And because German bonds are used as a benchmark for Europe, it  raises 
rates across all Eurozone countries. Is France the only country seeing its public debt go up?  
No, it's not, Emmanuel. This is happening in the United Kingdom and in the United States. These economies, like France, 
are running public deficits that are ever close or exceed 5% of GDP, which is too large for debt ratio to stabilize on its ow n. 
France and the United Kingdom are trying to gradually reduce their public deficit so as not to compromise growth. The 
United States is not going on that path yet, but market pressure could eventually force them to do so.  
0:12:08 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much for your input, Guillaume. Thank you, Emmanuel. Let's go to the next segment. It's about the impact 
of rising bond yields on financing and investments in the Eurozone.  
0:12:26 - Thomas Humblot 
Hello, Thomas. Welcome. Hello. Thank you for having me today.  
0:12:30 - Emmanuel Laborde 
We've just seen what's behind the rise in Eurozone bond yields. This rise is partly attributed to the sovereign spread. What 
impact will this have on the economy? 
0:12:42 - Thomas Humblot 
Actually, this widening of some government bond spreads is obviously not good news, but it tends paradoxically to have 
less impact than an increase in long-term interest rates that does not include the sovereign risk premium. for example, in 
France, for a very long time, it has been considered that the 10 -year OAT was the relevant benchmark for fixed loans. And 
at that time, the spread on French government bonds was relatively stable. But since 2024, that spread increased

### Page 5

5

significantly and that renders the sovereign yield a less relevant benchmark.  
And now the most relevant benchmark is the swap rate because it determines the cost of bank funding that backs new 
loans. 
0:13:56 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Since the end of 2024, we have seen a rise in long-term 10-year swap rates. What impact does this have on bank lending 
rates? 
0:14:08 - Thomas Humblot 
Fixed loans have obviously become more expensive, but to a lesser extent than if they were priced solely based on long -
term sovereign rates. And what about existing fixed-rate loans? 
There is no impact. An increase in rates for the entire maturity of a loan does not have any effect if it is a fixed -rate loan. 
Borrowers with outstanding loans are therefore protected. Obviously, new fixed loans are granted at higher rates. And this 
sharply contrasts with a floating-rates loans. 
Because with floating-rates loans, borrowers experience regular rate adjustments throughout the term of the loan. And in 
terms of bank financing? Strictly in terms of bank financing, an increase in short -term rates has an immediate and 
widespread restrictive impact on borrowers, while for long-term interest rates, the impact is more gradual, and it comes 
gradually as the outstanding amount of fixed-rate loans are progressively renewed. And the thing is that since the 
beginning of the war in Iran, we have seen a combination of the two.  
And in practical terms, the rate of new bank loans to non-financial corporations has increased about 25 basis points in the 
eurozone since the beginning of the year. 
0:16:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Just a few words about companies that borrow directly from the market.  
0:16:16 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, in that case, the impact is even more straightforward. The rate at which a company borrows from the market is the 
risk-free rate plus a premium. 
0:16:28 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Have risk premiums, known as corporate credit spreads, also increased?  
0:16:34 - Thomas Humblot 
Yes, they have increased temporarily at the beginning of the year but have returned to normal since. For the time being, the 
widening remains moderate for the highest-rated investment-grade issuers. 
However, some significant bond issuance in the euro debt market due to some major US technology companies has 
increased spreads for longer maturities. 
0:17:11 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In particular terms, how does this affect the investment decisions of the companies?  
0:17:19 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, investments that were previously worthwhile due to returns sufficient to cover the cost of capital may no longer do so 
and in turn that might lead to a decrease in demand for financing.  
0:17:40 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much Thomas. Now let us move on to the emerging economies. And it is with Cynthia Kalasopatan -Antoine.

### Page 6

6

Hello Cynthia, and welcome. 
0:17:54 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Hi, Emmanuel. Hello, everyone. 
0:17:57 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Long-term bond yields rose significantly in advanced economies over the summer. What can we now say about emerging 
markets? 
0:18:06 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Emerging bond markets held up well, which is quite unusual. The rise in long -term sovereign yields in emerging countries 
has been less pronounced than in advanced economies, including the United States and Germany. Now, there are two 
main exceptions, Turkey and Poland. Meanwhile, we did not witness any significant correction in emerging financial 
markets over the summer, and the main currencies appreciated slightly against the dollar or the euro.  
0:18:37 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Does this mean that public finances are more robust in emerging countries?  
0:18:43 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, it depends. You see, in cases like the Czech Republic and South Korea, public debt is moderate and macroeconomic 
fundamentals are solid. However, these countries are now less frequently considered as emerging and tend to be viewed 
as newly advanced economies. By contrast, Brazil and South Africa have weaker public finances and investors have 
already incorporated a higher sovereign risk into their assessments. And this is reflected in the bond yields. And this 
assessment of sovereign risk did not really change over the summer. 
0:19:26 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When comparing the main fiscal metrics, what are the trends? What do you see as an economist?  
0:19:33 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
On average, the fiscal metrics in emerging markets are not substantially worse than those in advanced economies. The 
average budget deficit levels are similar, ranging from 4% to 5% of GDP in 2025 -2026, according to the IMF. And as in 
advanced economies, the public debt is higher than its pre-Covid level. However, the average public debt to GDP ratio in 
emerging countries excluding China is close to 60%, whereas in advanced economies, it is above 100%.  
0:20:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Do you arrive at the same conclusion when comparing debt sustainability criteria?  
0:20:17 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, there are significant regional differences. In emerging Asia, the factors that determine debt sustainability are more 
often favorable than those in advanced economies. In Central Europe, the gap between the interest rate paid on debt and 
the GDP growth rate is also favorable. However, the primary deficits in some countries are high.  
0:20:45 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In a few words, a brief overview of the situation in Latin America.  
0:20:49 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, Brazil and Mexico are in a less favorable position. These countries would indeed need to generate a significant 
primary budget surplus each year in order to stabilize their debt in the medium term.

### Page 7

7

0:21:07 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Economists use the term original sin, which refers to the situation when governments resort to borrowing in foreign 
currency to address the shortfall in domestic financing. Does this phenomenon continue to distinguish emerging 
countries? 
0:21:24 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Yes, this is still the case. Emerging countries still have a significant share of their debt in foreign currency. This repres ents 
about 35 to 40% of their total debt on average. And in fact, now, this figure has decreased over the past decade, and this 
contributes to their resilience. However, governments in emerging markets have a less favorable position as regards their 
debt profile and their financing costs are also on average higher.  
0:22:02 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And finally, just a few words about a larger subject, China.  
0:22:07 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
China's public debt is significant and growing rapidly. That being said, it is denominated in domestic currency and issued in  
domestic markets. This debt is, however, sustainable in the medium term due to a very low effective interest rate and a 
high growth rate. 
0:22:29 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Cynthia, for being with us today.  
0:22:32 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Thank you. 
0:22:33 - Emmanuel Laborde 
It is now time to conclude, and we welcome back Group Chief Economist Isabelle Mateos y Lago.  
It is time for our conclusion with Isabelle. Once again, just a final question, please. Could or should, I do not know which 
word is the good one, central banks intervene to curb this rise in interest rates?  
0:22:58 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Absolutely not. The main contribution that central banks can bring to dealing with this situation is to restore price stabili ty, 
as is their mandate, as promptly as possible. And in the current circumstances, this means keeping relatively high policy 
rates, in some cases, rising them further. And the responsibility for keeping public finances on a sustainable footing rests 
squarely with governments and any attempt by these governments to instead take the easy way out by pressuring central 
banks to lower interest rates prematurely would be enormously counterproductive.  
0:23:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Isabelle. Thank you all for joining us today. Just a few words to remind you that additional economic 
research content can be found by scanning the QR code shown on your screen. Do not hesitate to use it with your 
smartphone. It is very easy. It was a pleasure to be with you today. Have a nice day and hope to see you so

## Document 243: IMF警告日本可能需要更快加息，全球债市再添压力？

Source PDF: all report pdf/IMF警告日本可能需要更快加息，全球债市再添压力？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-17

### Page 1

IMF 罕⻅背书⽇本货币政策正常化，并提⽰若通胀超预期央⾏需加快加息。⽬前⽇本政策利
率已升⾄ 1.25% ，市场预计年内或再加息。随着⽇债收益率上升，⽇本资⾦加速撤离法债
等海外资产，加剧了全球债市的波动与承压。
摘要：
国际货币基⾦组织罕⻅地为⽇本货币政策正常化背书，并警告若通胀超预期，⽇本央⾏可能需
要加快加息步伐。这⼀表态，在全球债市已然动荡之际，令市场对⽇本资⾦撤离海外资产的担
忧进⼀步升温。
IMF 亚太部⻔主任 Krishna Srinivasan 在本周于曼⾕召开的 IMF 年会期间接受路透社采访时表⽰，
⽇本央⾏ " 正在做正确的事 " ，并强调若通胀上升快于预期， " 必须更快收紧，因为你不希望通胀
预期脱锚。这取决于数据。 "⽇本央⾏⾏⻓植⽥和男亦在出席本次 IMF 年会时重申，央⾏将专注
于将核⼼通胀锚定在 2% ⽬标附近，不会容忍任何超调，进⼀步强化了市场对年内再度加息的预
期。
IMF 的表态恰逢全球债市承压之际。法国 10 年期国债收益率⼀度逼近 5% ，创 2002 年以来新⾼，

借贷成本已⾼于希腊和意⼤利；美国 10 年期国债收益率攀升⾄ 5.28% 的数⼗年⾼位，即便通胀
数据⾛软也未能遏制抛售势头。在部分市场分析⼈⼠看来，⽇本政策正常化及由此引发的⽇本
资⾦回流，或是这两场动荡背后不可忽视的结构性⼒量。
⽇本央⾏正常化提速预期升温
⽇本央⾏已于今年 6 ⽉和 9 ⽉相继加息，将政策利率推升⾄ 1.25% ，为 31 年来最⾼。多数分析师
预计年内还将有⾄少⼀次加息。
Srinivasan 指出，⽇元疲软进⼀步推⾼了原材料进⼝成本，加剧了国内通胀压⼒，这也正是促
使⽇本央⾏持续收紧政策的重要背景之⼀。⽇本央⾏本⽉晚些时候将召开关键政策会议，市场
普遍预计届时利率将维持不变，但可能释放鹰派信号，为 12 ⽉加息铺路。
此外，中东冲突带来的通胀⻛险和⼈⼯智能需求旺盛，也在本次 IMF 年会上成为各国政策制定者
讨论的重要议题。
IMF ：⽇元⼲预不违背浮动汇率承诺
在汇率问题上， IMF 对⽇本当局的⽴场给予背书。 Srinivasan 表⽰，⽇本当局 " 完全致⼒于⾃由浮

动汇率制度 " ，他与⽇⽅进⾏了多次讨论，对此 " 毫不怀疑 " 。
IMF 在其亚洲经济报告的分析章节中指出， 2025 年年中以来⽇元的⼤幅波动，很⼤程度上与收
益率差等基本⾯因素关联不⼤，⽽是与⽇元期货的投机性仓位同步移动。⽇本于今年 7 ⽉ 31 ⽇与
美国联合进⾏了⼀次罕⻅的协调⼲预，以阻⽌⽇元跌⾄近 40 年低点。 Srinivasan 表⽰，⽇本汇市
深度充⾜，当局将汇率波动作为 " 第⼀道防线 " ，⼲预仅在特定情况下使⽤。
除货币政策外，⽇本的财政可持续性同样令市场存有疑虑。市场担⼼⽇本政府的⼤规模⽀出计
划，叠加明年 4 ⽉起为期两年的消费税下调，将进⼀步扩⼤本已庞⼤的债务规模。
Srinivasan 承认市场对财政⻛险的担忧，但也指出，疫情以来⽇本的财政整合⼒度实际上已超过
其他任何⼀个 G7 国家，部分原因在于税收收⼊的持续增⻓。他同时强调，⽇本需要明确新⽀出

⽇本资⾦撤离或加剧全球债市波动
全球债市的动荡，与⽇本政策正常化进程之间的关联正受到越来越多的关注。据彭博专栏作家
Gearoid Reidy 在分析⽂章中指出，世界或许并未为 " 有利率的⽇本 " 做好充分准备，⽽法国可能
只是第⼀个受到冲击的市场。
数据显⽰，截⾄今年 7 ⽉，⽇本投资者持有约 23 万亿⽇元（约合 1450 亿美元）法国国债，是⽇本
在欧元区最⼤的超配仓位。然⽽随着⽇本 10 年期国债收益率上⽉升破 3% ，创 30 年新⾼，经汇率
对冲后，法国 10 年期国债相对⽇本国债的收益率优势已收窄⾄约 40 个基点，吸引⼒⼤幅下滑。
收益率优势消退叠加法国财政状况恶化，⽇本资⾦已开始松动。数据显⽰，⽇本持有的法国国

债规模较去年底减少 2.5% 。三井住友 DS 资产管理旗下由 Shinji Kunibe 负责的全球固收基⾦已出
于财政担忧清仓法债。瑞穗的 Masayuki Nakajima 表⽰： " 即便估值看起来更便宜，⽇本投资者
重建仓位的动⼒也在下降。 "
随着 IMF 为⽇本加快加息提供政策⽀撑，⽇本国内资产吸引⼒的持续回升，或将使这⼀趋势延
续，并对依赖⽇本资⾦的海外债券市场形成持续的结构性压⼒。

THE WEEKLY AGGREGATES REPORT
The Weekly Aggregates report provides updates on aggregate earnings revisions, growth rates and valuations. The 
numbers in the "Global Datapoints", "U.S. Datapoints" and "Ancillary Data" sections are updated weekly.
This report combines I/B/E/S's unrivaled historical earnings database, in depth coverage of Wall Street analysts' 
bottom-up corporate earnings estimates, and the analytic capabilities of the Proprietary Research group 
and desk-top solutions.

WEEKLY AGGREGATES
ITEM DEFINITIONS AND METHODOLOGY
3© 2026 LSEG.  All marks herein are used under licence. TRPR_82222_867
General:
All aggregates are bottom-up aggregates calculated from analysts' consensus forecasts unless otherwise noted. Bottom-up
aggregates are weighted calculations using the I/B/E/S data from LSEG share class monitored or the official index weight of
the index-provider. Top-down forecasts are compilations of strategists' estimates. I/B/E/S universe calculations are based on

all companies within a given market monitored by LSEG.
Benchmark index calculations, including those for official local market indices, the S&P 500 Index, and the FTSE World Equity
Indices are calculated using official index constituents and weightings. All index composites are representative of current
index constituents. Index constituents and weightings are updated monthly during options expiration (the Thursday before the
third Friday of every month) for every index except the S&P 500, the constituents of which are current as of the date of

calculation.
PEIGBY refers to the Price Earnings Inverse Government Bond Yield ratio, which measures equity valuation with respect to
the inverse-yield on long-term government bonds (now known as the 'Fed' model). All PEIGBY ratios are I/B/E/S universe-
specific except for the US ratio, where it is based on the S&P 500 Index.
Methodology:
For US and selected Canadian aggregates, calendar-year aggregation is performed using the sum of four quarters' estimates

and a quarterly pro-ration is applied to non-December year end companies' quarterly EPS estimates. Outside of the US and
Canada, all company-level data are calendarized prior to aggregation using the Compustat method of calendarization.
Aggregation is performed using the annual consensus EPS estimate and a May roll-back is applied to December year-end
markets. Aggregates for Japan, where most companies report in March, use an August rollback and a November rollback is

used for Australia and New Zealand, where the fiscal year-end for most companies is in June.
All 12-month-forward data for ex-US markets are calculated using a pro-rata weighting to annual EPS forecasts. The
weightings applied are a daily calibration out of 365 days relative to each constituent's individual fiscal year-end and the
current date.
All non-surprise data, including aggregate forecast earnings, forecast growth, and forward-looking valuation calculations are

weighted according to the I/B/E/S share class monitored for I/B/E/S universes or the weightings assigned to each constituent
in a given index. Aggregated earnings calculations are the equivalent of net profit aggregates, as they are derived from the
EPS forecasts aggregated to the net income level by the appropriate share weighting.
Earnings Aggregation:
For 12-month-forward earnings, the 12-month-forward EPS is used in place of the calendarized EPS in the formulas below.

I/B/E/S universe EPS Aggregate = Index EPS Aggregate =
Growth Calculations:
All growth calculations are the weighted year over year growth in earnings for the calendarized fiscal year period. The
formulas below use index shares for index growth calculations.
Calendarized Earnings Growth = 12-month-forward Growth =
Valuation:
All valuation calculations are the weighted-average price-earnings ratio for the fiscal period in question. The formulas below

use index shares for index P/E calculations.
Calendarized P/E Aggregate = 12-month-forward P/E =
Revisions Momentum:
Unless otherwise indicated, analysts' revisions momentum is a simple aggregation of the total number of estimates raised
divided by the total number of estimates lowered. Thus, any revisions ratio less than 1 indicates negative revisions
momentum while a ratio greater than 1 indicates positive revisions momentum, with 1 marking parity.

The Global Aggregates from LSEG product contains annual aggregates for universes included in this publication, including
supplemental aggregate data of over 100 items as well as history dating back to 1985 for North America and to 1987 for other
markets. To learn more about this product, contact your LSEG sales representative.
FTSE and the FTSE World Equity Indices are owned by FTSE International Limited.

## Document 270: Oil prices hold firm despite Persian Gulf supply growing

Source PDF: all report pdf/Oil prices hold firm despite Persian Gulf supply growing.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Oil prices remain ﬁrm despite rising supply from the Persian
Gulf. We expect oil prices to trade lower into year-end. More
positive signs of a potential US-Iran deal would give us more
conﬁdence in this view. Meanwhile, European gas looks
vulnerable heading deeper into the heating season
Warren Patterson
Oil prices are holding ﬁrm despite Persian Gulf supply increasing
Persian Gulf oil ﬂows pick-up
There are few signs of de-escalation in the Middle East. If anything,

it appears as though the conﬂict is broadening, with the Houthis in
Yemen increasingly targeting Saudi Arabia. Despite little
improvement in the geopolitical environment, oil ﬂows from the
Persian Gulf have been trending higher with supply from the region
returning to 70-75% of pre-war levels, although there are some
estimates suggesting we could be back to as high as 80-90% of
pre-war ﬂows.

### Page 2

The fact that oil prices are well-supported despite the improvement
in ﬂows from the region appears to reﬂect concerns over how
sustainable this supply is, particularly when considering the
geopolitical backdrop. The market continues to price in a large risk
premium in the absence of a deal between the US and Iran. And to
see this risk premium erode, the market may need the comfort of a
deal. Failing that, time may be enough for oil prices to trend lower,

assuming supplies from the region continue to hold up. For now, we
are holding onto our 4Q26 Brent forecast of $80/bbl and FY27
forecast of $71/bbl. However, given the high level of uncertainty,
this is a low-conviction call, speciﬁcally for 4Q26.
As oil ﬂows from the Persian Gulf have picked up, another
bottleneck has become increasingly noticeable in the oil market.
There is signiﬁcant tightness in the tanker market, which has seen
tanker freight rates surge to record levels. Oil ﬂows from the Persian

Gulf have become increasingly ineﬃcient due to the challenging
environment, which has absorbed more of the global tanker ﬂeet.
And the only way this starts to normalise is if the way oil ﬂows
from the region also starts to normalise.
The other pain point for the oil market remains diesel, which will be
a concern as we head further into the Northern Hemisphere winter.
Disruptions from the Persian Gulf and Russia have tightened the
market, while the risk of export restrictions from the US has kept the

market nervous. The risk of US export restrictions appears to have
subsided, with Europe set to release diesel from strategic reserves.
Furthermore, expensive freight suggests that reﬁned product prices
are likely to remain elevated.
Natural gas vulnerable heading into heating
season
European natural gas prices have traded at their highest level since
December 2022, with supply concerns growing as the region heads
into the heating season. Unfortunately for the natural gas market,

we have not seen a similar recovery in LNG ﬂows from the Persian
Gulf as we have with oil. Therefore, LNG ﬂows from the region
remain down about 80% year-on-year. The market is increasingly

This publication has been prepared by ING solely for information purposes
irrespective of a particular user's means, ﬁnancial situation or investment
accepting that Persian Gulf LNG could remain largely absent from
global markets for much of the Northern Hemisphere winter. This
means Asia and Europe will continue competing for spot cargoes
throughout the winter, keeping prices well supported and leaving
the market vulnerable to further spikes.
European gas storage remains a concern, at just under 75% full,

well below the ﬁve-year average of 89%. Low storage has seen the
German government instruct state-owned SEFE to buy and store an
additional 8TWh of natural gas by 15 December. The prospect of
essentially forced buying provides further support to the market.
With little sign of an imminent recovery in Persian Gulf LNG supply,
weather will be crucial this winter, and there is growing expectation
that Europe will experience a winter of milder temperatures due to

El Niño, which would provide some relief to the market. However, it
is not a strong relationship, and El Niño does not guarantee milder
temperatures. The North Asia winter will also be important for the
LNG market, as El Niño can also typically mean milder
temperatures in this region.
We now expect TTF to average EUR73/MWh over the 26/27 winter,
up from a previous forecast of EUR65/MWh. The market will likely be
very volatile over this period, given the lower starting point for gas

storage. Prices are then expected to sell oﬀ aggressively from 2Q27
as Persian Gulf supply normalises, and we see a further ramp-up in
US LNG export capacity.

## Document 323: USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%

Source PDF: all report pdf/USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

Personal consumption (third estimate) +3.8% (qoq ar) for Q2 vs. GS +3.4%, median
forecast +3.4%, prior revised +0.7%
Personal income +0.2% for August vs. GS +0.5%, median forecast +0.5%, prior revised
+0.3%
Personal spending +0.9% for August vs. GS +0.8%, median forecast +0.9%, prior revised
+0.1%
Advance goods trade balance -$132.6bn for August vs. GS -$116.0bn, median forecast
-$115.3bn, prior revised -$118.9bn
ADP employment +90k for September vs. GS +75k, median forecast +75k, prior revised

+36k
MAIN POINTS:
1. The core PCE index increased +0.25% in August, somewhat below expectations, and
the year-over-year rate increased 3.01%. We estimate that the Dallas Fed’s trimmed
mean PCE measure increased 0.16% month over month and 2.20% year over year.
2. Year-over-year core PCE was revised downward 0.36pp in July to 2.98%, primarily
reflecting the methodological revisions to the calculation of the portfolio management
(-0.23pp), computer accessories and software (-0.11pp), and legal services (+0.01pp)

components of PCE. These downward revisions were larger than we expected, mostly
reflecting a larger revision to the portfolio management component than we expected.
3. Today’s inflation report, especially the downward revisions resulting from the
methodological changes, imply that core PCE inflation is likely to substantially
undershoot the median FOMC participant’s forecast of 3.4% for 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis.
We now expect a 3.0% increase for core PCE in 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis. Coupled with

comments from New York Fed President John Williams yesterday that “with the policy
action we took at our September meeting, there is no need for urgency, and we have
time to gather more information,” we now think that an October hike is unlikely.  We are
pushing back the second hike in our forecast to December, and we see a strong chance
that the FOMC will ultimately conclude that additional rate hikes are unnecessary.
4. Real GDP was revised up by 0.7pp to +2.2% in Q2 (quarter-over-quarter annualized),

largely reflecting upward revisions to consumption (+0.4pp to +3.8%) and investment
(+1.9pp to +4.6%) growth. The strength in consumer spending in Q2 likely continued to
reflect the boost to consumer cashflow from larger-than-usual tax refunds, but we
continue to expect consumer spending growth to slow somewhat in 2026Q4 as the fiscal
support fades and the headwind from higher energy prices remains. Stronger
investment growth reflected an upward revision to private fixed investment (+0.5pp to

+9.0%), driven by data center investment in the nonresidential structures category
(+1.9pp to +0.1%), and an upward revision to the contribution from inventories (+0.2pp
to -0.5pp).
5. Reflecting today’s annual revisions to the national accounts, real GDP was revised up
by 0.4pp to +2.5% in Q1 (quarter-over-quarter annualized), largely reflecting upward
revisions to the contributions from consumption and exports. Revisions for prior years
were modest: real GDP growth on a Q4/Q4 basis was unrevised for 2025 (+2.0%) and

2023 (+3.4%), and slightly revised down for 2024 (-0.2pp to +2.4%) and up for 2022
30 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs

### Page 3

(+0.1pp to +1.3%). 
6. Personal income increased 0.2% in August, below expectations. Year-over-year 
disposable personal income growth reached 4.8% in August. Personal spending rose 
0.9% in August, in line with consensus expectations. The savings rate decreased 0.5pp to 
4.1% in August, after a large 1.6pp upward revision to the savings rate last month driven 
by an upward revision to the level of disposable personal income (+1.2pp contribution) 
and a downward revision to personal spending as a share of DPI (+0.4pp). 
7. According to the Advance Economic Indicators Report, the goods trade deficit 
widened by $13.7bn to $132.6bn (seasonally adjusted) in August, well above 
expectations. Goods exports increased by $3.7bn to $203.4bn, largely driven by an 
increase in industrial supplies exports. Goods imports increased by $17.4bn to 
$336.1bn, driven by a large increase in industrial supplies and capital goods imports. 
8. According to the ADP report, private sector employment rose by 90k in September 
(month-over-month, seasonally adjusted), slightly above consensus expectations. 
Employment in the services sector rose 59k, primarily reflecting a 55k increase in 
education and health services and a 22k increase in leisure and hospitality employment 
that were partly offset by a 16k decrease in financial activities employment. 
Employment in goods-producing industries increased by 31k, driven by a 17k increase in 
manufacturing and a 15k increase in construction employment. ADP’s new median 
annual base pay measure, which better tracks other measures of wage growth than its 
gross pay measure, increased 3.0% for job stayers, unchanged from the August pace, 
and 4.8% for job changers, 0.1pp above the August pace. We left our forecast for August 
nonfarm payroll growth unchanged at +80k (vs. +90k consensus) ahead of Friday’s 
release. 
9. The details of the personal income and spending report were firmer than our previous 
GDP tracking assumptions. While the trade-related details of the Advance Economic 
Indicators report were much weaker than our previous assumptions, suggesting notably 
stronger imports growth in Q3 than we previously assumed, the inventories details were 
significantly stronger. On net, we lowered our Q3 GDP tracking estimate by 0.1pp to 
+3.3% (quarter-over-quarter annualized).

## Document 326: Westpac Card Tracker, 2 October 2026 (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Westpac Card Tracker, 2 October 2026 (2026-10-02).pdf

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
2
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
• The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index* has edged up slightly since late
August, rising 0.2pts to 159.1.
• Quarterly growth momentum has
seen a further lift and is now tracking
towards a 1.7% gain for the September
quarter which would mark the strongest

quarterly rise since June 2025.
• However, both the monthly and weekly
reads show momentum has faded over
the course of the quarter. After posting
0.7-0.9%mth gains through May, June
and July, the Index rose just 0.2%
in August and is tracking a similarly
subdued 0.3% pace for September.
Weekly reads are also tracking a more
moderate trend pace of 0.2%mth.
• On a quarterly basis, the detail shows
discretionary services and international
transactions have led the pick-up.

While some of this looks to be catch-
up from travel disruptions in the June
quarter, gains have been consistent and
broadly based. Likewise, there is little
evidence that the pick-up is coming from
a lift in prices with recent CPI updates
suggesting services inflation has been
steady rather than accelerating (see
here).
• On the apparent loss of momentum
over the course of the quarter, the detail
suggests this is a re-run of the fuel price
squeeze in March-April-May with the

essentials spend gain rising strongly. So
far the spillover effects are showing up
mainly as a dampening in discretionary
goods spend with the slowing in
discretionary services largely confined to
hospitality.
• Overall, the card tracker data suggests
the Q3 spending data will still come in
as a relatively solid gain, even allowing
for a firmer gain in prices in the quarter.
However, the fade in momentum is
notable with the impact of further fuel

pricer increases and this week’s RBA rate
rise still to come. Add what now looks
more likely to be a follow-on interest
rate hike in November and the December
quarter is shaping as a significant
slowdown in card activity.
“... both the monthly
and weekly reads show
momentum has faded over
the course of the quarter.”

rise in nominal terms and price rise in the

1%qtr range points to a real, inflation
adjusted gain of around 0.7%qtr, in line
with Westpac’s current forecasts.

• Our MHSI and retail card indexes

MHSI index measures significantly 
outperformed the broader WCTI in

show both more in line at 1.6%qtr and 
1.7%qtr respectively. 
• The retail card index measure is a 
narrower subset of spend that excludes 
fuel and most services except hospitality. 
Chart 6 shows a sharp weakening in 
non food retail in recent weeks with 
food retail also stalling but hospitality 
tracking a firmer, albeit slowing pace.
